# Training Run Log

Concise record of every Slurm training job: why it was submitted, and why it stopped.
Update this table whenever a job is submitted or cancelled.

**Standing context (07-22, user directive):** we are currently in the
EXPLORATION phase, not the exploitation phase. The purpose of every arm right
now is to learn how to correctly configure a future, longer/bigger "final"
production run -- NOT to reach the optimal model in the current sweep. Once
an arm's core question is answered (a lesson is learned, whether positive or
negative), cancel it and launch the next idea immediately, even if it is far
short of its num_steps cap and even if it looks "fine" -- do not let jobs run
just because they haven't failed. Only once we have converged on a
well-understood, reliable hyperparameter configuration should we shift to
running fewer, longer, bigger runs (the exploitation phase).

| — | **Quantified the real-continuation catastrophic-overlap failure mode (~35-40% of examples) + shipped 3 infra fixes: best-by-turn-taking-probe checkpoint tracking, auto-resolving eval checkpoint selection, configurable floor_control category weights** | 08-28 12:5x (user asked: reward redesign ideas, use best not final checkpoint for eval, quantify+show best/worst weakness examples) | **Quantitative weakness measurement**: wrote `scripts/analysis/rank_best_worst_continuations.py` (composite score = strict_handoff - dirty - 0.5*interruption - overlap_rate - repetition_gate, per model-side example) and ran it against every finished wave52 `per_example_metrics.csv`. Finding: 25-40% of examples per seed (baseline=28%, dynstate_gru_balcecfg seeds mostly 37-40%, except the original leader seed 176106 at 25%) land in a "catastrophic" bucket (score < -1.0), and it's dominated by runaway OVERLAP (13-16 of each seed's catastrophic cases have overlap_rate>0.3, vs only 0-2 from repetition collapse) -- e.g. `final52_h100_dynbal_s273106` trial `trial_8bbdeb4826730ece9481` (AMI): human overlap_rate=0.01 vs model=0.77, with a literal `"a a a a a a a..."` repetition spiral; several werewolf/meld examples show 2 agents both generating substantial, sometimes near-identical, simultaneous text. **This means the new-recipe's broader seed sample trends WORSE than the plain baseline on this specific real-generation failure axis**, even though it's clearly better on synthetic broad-sweep clean_handoff_rate -- needs a formal paired statistical comparison before concluding this is a genuine regression vs. noise, but it's concerning enough to flag now rather than wait. **Best-checkpoint-for-eval fix**: `CheckpointManager` (`training/checkpoint.py`) already tracked a val-loss-best checkpoint (root `model_state.pt`, separate from terminal `last/`), but every final-run eval so far pointed `CHECKPOINT_DIR` at `.../last` (the worst of the 3 choices) -- added a NEW, independent `on_probe()`-tracked `best_probe/` checkpoint (scored by `training/race.py::probe_quality_score`, a composite of the already-computed in-training `run_kitchen_sink_probe` fields: clean_handoff_rate, nonoverlap_listener_rate, overlap_rate, unwanted_interruption_rate, repeated_4gram_fraction -- no extra generation cost), wired into `main.py`'s training loop right after each probe. `scripts/final_runs/evaluate_final_checkpoint.sbatch` now auto-resolves at runtime (`CHECKPOINT_PREFER=auto`, default): `best_probe/` > root (best val_loss) > `last/`; `launch_portfolio.py` now passes the checkpoint ROOT instead of hardcoding `/last`. **Reward reweighting lever**: added `weight_silence/same/handoff/overlap` to `FloorControlConfig` (all default 1.0 = exactly reproduces the old unweighted-mean behavior) plus matching `--floor_control_weight_*` CLI flags -- lets a future run prioritize the OVERLAP category's calibration signal above its natural ~7%-of-columns share, directly targeting the failure mode just quantified, without touching the proper-scoring/anti-exploit properties of each category's own score (see updated docstrings in `model/turn_reward.py`). NOT yet tried in a real run -- this is a queued, ready-to-launch lever, not a confirmed fix; the deeper issue is likely exposure bias (model never trains on recovering from ITS OWN overlap mistakes under pure teacher forcing), which only the still-pending Tier 2/3 rollout-based reward work can fully address. Added 8 new tests (checkpoint best-probe tracking, probe_quality_score, floor_control_reward category weighting) -- full suite 459/459 passing. | DONE (infra); reward-reweighting experiment not yet launched | Next: queue a `floor_control_weight_overlap=2.0-3.0` arm against the current leader recipe once GPU budget frees up, and re-run `rank_best_worst_continuations.py` against its held-out eval to see if the catastrophic-overlap rate actually drops |
| — | **CRITICAL incident: ~128 source files (main.py, model/, data/adapters/, evaluation/, scripts/final_runs+ops+data_prep+training, tests/, docs, SUCCESS_STORIES.md, 5x .cursor/rules/*.mdc) mass-deleted from disk; recovered via NetApp `.snapshot`, zero data loss** | 08-28 12:3x (routine checkup, triggered by user's "h100 not being used") | User asked for a status update and flagged H100 sitting idle. Investigation found `scripts/ops/` (job_queue.py, gpu_yield_daemon.py) and `main.py` genuinely missing from disk -- not a daemon bug, the files themselves were gone, even though 4 `mc_data_ablation` jobs launched hours earlier kept running fine (already-loaded processes don't need the source file). `git status` was a red herring (repo's only commit is a stale 07-11 snapshot, 6+ weeks before this incident, so it shows everything since as "deleted" regardless). Root cause of the actual deletion is unknown (not attributable to any command run this session) but scope/timing is precisely bounded: diffed the live tree against `/home/.../. snapshot/hourly.2026-08-28_1705` (NetApp hourly snapshot, ~28min old at detection time) and found exactly 128 files present in the snapshot and absent live, zero files present live but absent from the snapshot -- i.e. nothing created since that snapshot, only deletions, and the deletion window is bounded to the ~30min between the 1705 snapshot and detection. Restored all 128 files via `rsync -a --ignore-existing` (snapshot -> live, never overwriting anything still present) -- confirmed zero remaining tree diff afterward. Verified integrity: `py_compile` clean on every restored .py, zero real null bytes (byte-level check, not the false-positive-prone `grep $'\x00'`), and **full test suite 452/452 passing** post-restore. TRAINING_RUN_LOG.md/SUCCESS_STORIES.md were among the restored files and are confirmed intact through their last real entries (this file's table is newest-first; nothing between 08-27 15:28 and now was lost since nothing was added in that window before the deletion). | RESOLVED | If this recurs, check `~/.snapshot/hourly.*` immediately -- NetApp hourly snapshots on this filesystem are the fastest recovery path and were sufficient here |

| 274174 | mc_reward_balcecfg (rewardredesign_balanced_ce_s176106) | 08-24 14:32 | Treatment arm: IDENTICAL recipe/seed to 274173 except `turn_reward_mode=balanced_ce` (new `model/turn_reward.py::floor_control_reward` -- balanced 4-way proper-scoring cross-entropy over silence/same/handoff/overlap, no hand-tuned shape/decay parameters). Rearchitected per user request (08-24) to fix two structural issues in the old linear reward: (1) the overlap penalty taxes any transitional probability mass immediately (no true grace period, unlike silence), so the continuous relaxation's necessary path from "confidently same" to "confidently handoff" passes through a penalized region -- discouraging handoff attempts from the outset; (2) `speak_grace`/`speak_tau` import an RL-style time-constant decay into what is actually a per-column mean loss with no episode-level credit assignment. New design: 4 predicted probabilities (silence/same/handoff/overlap) are an exact simplex partition scored via cross-entropy (a strictly proper scoring rule) against the true per-column category, balanced equally across categories (mirrors existing balanced activity BCE) so the ~78%-common SAME class can't drown out HANDOFF's gradient, and reported as a log-likelihood-ratio against a fixed reference distribution (positive = beats naive baseline, negative = worse) per user's "mean reward term, above is positive/below is negative" framing. Defensively analyzed against the joint-multi-agent-activation exploit that made the (already-disabled-by-default) `same_handoff_cross_entropy` gameable -- verified via a dedicated regression test (`test_floor_control_reward_punishes_multi_claim_hedging`) that hedging by raising other agents' P(speak) strictly lowers reward when ground truth is SAME. Full new test suite (7 tests) + full existing suite (361 total) pass. Both arms verified RUNNING, zero tracebacks, correct hyperparameters landed (only `turn_reward_mode`/`handoff_bonus_weight` differ as expected). 2 H100 GPUs used (well within the 4-GPU cap; 1 GPU already in use by another user on `dh-dgxh100-1`, not disturbed). | RUNNING | Compare `clean_handoff_rate`/`chain_2plus_rate`/decoded text at matching probe steps (250/500/.../2000) against 274173 |

| — | **Bug fix: `sbatch --export=ALL,K=V,...` silently corrupts comma-containing queue-entry env values** | 08-26 14:20 (found while preparing to queue a `DATASET_WEIGHTS` ablation) | `job_queue.py`-driven `submit_queue_entry`/`resubmit` in `gpu_yield_daemon.py` built `--export=` by joining `entry["env"]` with commas -- exactly the documented `.cursor/rules/sbatch-env-overrides.mdc` hazard (Slurm's `--export` parser splits on every comma regardless of quoting), which would have silently truncated any queued job's `DATASET_WEIGHTS="a=0.4,b=0.12,..."` override into garbage. Fixed both call sites to pass overrides via the subprocess's own `env=` kwarg with a bare `--export=ALL` instead of inlining. Added a regression test and verified against 3 real launches (274222-274224) that `DATASET_WEIGHTS` landed byte-for-byte correct in both stdout and `hyperparameters/*.json`. | FIXED | — |

Investigation confirmed the "90.5% same / 9.5% handoff" figure used to calibrate `same_handoff_cross_entropy` (and thus `reward_human_matched`'s `lambda_same_handoff=0.2` loss) is a naive PER-COLUMN marginal dominated by trivial mid-utterance token continuations (a 30-token utterance contributes ~29 non-decision "same" pairs and 1 real decision). Built `scripts/analysis/analyze_human_run_boundary_topology.py` to measure the CORRECT, run-boundary-conditioned statistic (collapse each utterance into one event, resolve forward through silence/overlap to find who becomes the next sole speaker) -- result is INVERTED: 83.6% handoff / 16.4% self-resume, not 9.5%/90.5%. | — | — | 07-20 (diagnostics) | Probed J/K/L/M (steep decay) and re-tested C_lora_only/M at lower activity_threshold (0.35/0.30/0.25/0.20) and with an in-context "primed handoff" scenario (demonstrate one handoff, see if a second follows). All negative: J/K/L/M show the SAME threshold-vs-overlap tradeoff curve as plain C_lora_only (no improvement from training-side reward reshaping); primed context does not increase handoff likelihood either. Conclusion: 8 different Stage-A reward-shape variants (F-M) all plateau at the same content-loss/behavior floor -- points to exposure bias (ground-truth-teacher-forced reward/activity training vs. free-running generation), not reward weighting, as the real bottleneck. | — | — |

| — | — | 07-20 19:28 (finding) | W/X/Y all show genuine 2-point POSITIVE trends: no_listener_response dropping (1.0->0.8, 0.8->0.6, 1.0->0.8 respectively), overlap staying modest (0.0-0.2, far below O/V's 0.6-0.85 collapse level), repetition flat-to-improving, calibration delta rising. X's step-2000 decoded text is a genuinely MIXED signal though -- 1/4 overlap cases is a near-exact echo (collapse-like), 1/4 is a new degenerate mode (laughter-token spam), 2/4 show real if repetitive content variation between agents. Not a clean collapse like O/V, but not unambiguously healthy either -- watching closely whether the bad-pattern fraction shrinks (progress) or grows (heading to collapse) over the next 1-2 checkpoints. Z (0.7+overlap) shows no engagement yet at its only point so far (step 1500), similar early profile to N before N's brief bump; too early to judge. |
| — | — | 07-20 (BUG FIX, major) | Found and fixed a real bug in `clean_handoff` scoring (scripts/evaluate_checkpoint_suite.py): it compared the listener's first spoken step against the reference speaker's FIRST spoken step instead of their LAST, silently mis-scoring the common genuinely-clean case (reference speaks briefly, stops, listener replies later) as "not clean" -- e.g. reference speaks only at step 0, listener starts at step 3: old check `3<=0`=False (wrong), new check `3>0`=True (correct). This was found via a user-requested demo of P_kitchen_sink's best example (documented in CLEAN_HANDOFFS_MATRIX.txt (Example 1)). Re-verified on the same checkpoint with the fix: clean_handoff_rate went from 0.0 to 0.1 -- the FIRST non-zero clean_handoff_rate recorded in the entire race. Added 4 regression tests (tests/test_checkpoint_probe.py). All future probe-based decisions should use the corrected metric; historical "clean_handoff_rate=0.0 everywhere" claims for completed arms were likely undercounts and should be re-verified opportunistically. | — | — |

The earlier "0.1 clean_handoff_rate" win (CLEAN_HANDOFFS_MATRIX.txt (Example 1)) was real but NOT representative -- it is an occasional lucky occurrence at specific prompt/seed/agent-count combinations, not a reliably learned skill; even the best checkpoint in the race leaves listeners silent ~94% of the time under broader testing. SEPARATE finding from the same data: the model leaks Werewolf-game vocabulary (voting language, random synthetic player names like Rachel/Julian/Dmitri/Mason) into content regardless of prompt topic -- a distinct content-overfitting signature worth tracking independently of the handoff-timing question. |

| — | — | 07-20 (feature: gradient_accumulation_steps) | User asked for a larger-batch-size experiment to help stability/overfitting. Implemented `--gradient_accumulation_steps` (default 1, backward compatible) instead of literally raising --batch_size, to get the same statistical smoothing benefit without the peak-memory risk we specifically fought hard to avoid on the V100 earlier in this project. Restructured the training loop in main.py (window-based zero_grad/step/scheduler.step, loss scaled by 1/accum_steps, throughput metrics summed across the window) plus fixed BATCH_SIZE being hardcoded (not overridable) in train_meld_ami_werewolf.sbatch, same bug pattern as before. Smoke-tested fresh run (steps/epoch correctly halved with accum=2, exactly N optimizer steps taken) AND resume-from-checkpoint (correctly resumed at step=4 batch=8, i.e. 4 steps x 2 micro-batches) on the real cluster before use. |
| — | — | 07-20 (feature: AMI laughter removal) | User asked to remove laughter tokens from "Friends" -- corrected: laughter comes from AMI's adapter, not MELD/Friends (MELD's adapter has no vocal-event handling at all). Removed the "laugh"->"[laughter]" mapping in data/adapters/ami.py (kept cough/breath) since real generation demos showed the model latching onto it as a cheap, content-free way to become "active", producing degenerate "[laughter] [laughter] [laughter]" spam instead of real dialogue. Added a regression test (tests/test_ami_adapter.py). Reprocessed into a NEW directory ($HOME/matrixchat/processed_meld_ami_nolaugh, job 267949) rather than overwriting the directory the other 6 running jobs are actively reading from. Verified via exact token-sequence search: 0/2309 AMI examples contain the "[laughter]" sequence post-fix (was previously present). |

| — | — | 07-22 08:35 (bug: missing NUM_STEPS cap, affects ~20 arms) | User asked for a deliberately aggressive experiment (boost speak-weight heavily, ignore metrics, just see a qualitatively different outcome). Cancelled SS (weakest current signal, best checkpoint step=500/val=3.26 preserved) to free the slot and launched activity_pos_weight=0.95 (well past the known 0.8 collapse threshold) + max_grad_norm=1.0. While checking its startup log, noticed `total planned steps=74535` instead of the usual ~5000 -- investigation revealed `NUM_STEPS` defaults to 0 (uncapped, runs the full num_epochs=15 -> 74535 steps, or until the 60h SBATCH wall-clock limit) in train_meld_ami_werewolf.sbatch, and comparing hyperparameter JSONs confirmed EVERY arm launched since GG (roughly 20+ arms: GG through YY) was missing an explicit `NUM_STEPS=5000` override that all earlier arms (AA-EE, F-Z, N-U, BB, etc.) had. This is a real oversight in my own launch commands, not a script bug -- the sbatch script's `${NUM_STEPS:-0}` pattern is correctly overridable, I simply stopped passing it. Currently-running long jobs (KK at 24h+/step 5480+, RR/SS/PP at 15h+, UU/VV/WW at 13-17h) were NOT restarted -- too much GPU time already invested, and their best_checkpoint.json selection is unaffected by the missing cap, so cross-run comparisons on best-val-loss remain valid regardless of how long each ran. Going forward, `NUM_STEPS=5000 NUM_EPOCHS=2` will be included in every new launch. |

Added `main.py`'s `run_kitchen_sink_probe()` closure (model.eval()+no_grad()+model.train() around the call, matching `evaluate()`'s pattern) and new CLI args `--probe_every` (default 0, disabled), `--probe_num_agents`, `--probe_scaling_agent_counts`, `--probe_max_new_tokens`, `--probe_include_secret_scenarios`; results logged into `metrics.jsonl` under a new `"probe"` key. Added a new `probe.png` plot to `scripts/analysis/plot_metrics.py` (auto-generated whenever a run used `--probe_every`). Wired `PROBE_EVERY`/`PROBE_NUM_AGENTS`/`PROBE_MAX_NEW_TOKENS` as overridable env vars into `train_meld_ami_werewolf.sbatch`. Added 2 new tests (`run_probe_suite` end-to-end against the tiny CPU model, with and without the scaling sweep) -- full suite 193/193 passing. |
| — | — | 07-27 (false-alarm debugging, ~2h) | GPU smoke-testing the new feature appeared to hang indefinitely (100% GPU util, zero new output, repeatedly killed by SLURM time limits across 9 attempts) even after ruling out DataLoader worker forking, `TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM`, gradient checkpointing, LoRA, and cluster node contention (reproduced identically on a completely idle node) as causes. Root cause turned out to be a complete red herring, already documented: `main.py` unconditionally runs a FULL validation pass at `step == 1` regardless of `--eval_every` (`.cursor/rules/first-step-validation-is-slow.mdc`, ~20 min over the real ~1000-example validation set) -- every smoke test attempt left `--val_fraction` non-zero with only a 10-20 min `srun --time` budget, so step 1's pre-existing slow validation silently ate the entire budget before the new probe code ever ran. Setting `--val_fraction 0` immediately produced a clean, fast, fully-correct run (probe fired at steps 1/2/4 as configured, ~0.7s/prompt, ~14s per probe call, training completed normally). Extended the cursor rule with an explicit "smoke tests fall into this trap too" section so this specific multi-hour detour isn't repeated. |

| — | — | 07-28 10:45 (user question: p0/p_same/p_handoff/p_overlap trend + new plot) | User noticed BB's step-1 `p_handoff_mean` (0.32, train) looked like the "best" value, seemingly dropping drastically afterward, and asked how these are measured / whether something's wrong. Explained the mechanism (`model/turn_reward.py`): these 4 values are a PARTITION of the model's own predicted per-agent P(speak) into mutually exclusive outcomes (silence / same-speaker-continues / handoff-to-different-speaker / overlap) that always sum to ~1 -- not 4 independent measurements. Step 1's `train_*` numbers are a single noisy micro-batch (batch_size=1) on an essentially untrained model and are not meaningful/comparable on their own. Added a new `reward_breakdown.png` plot (`scripts/analysis/plot_metrics.py`) showing smoothed train + full-validation-set values for all 4 -- the val curve (far less noisy, ~1500 examples averaged) tells the REAL story: p_overlap starts catastrophically high on the untrained model (96.5% at step 1) and training rapidly teaches it down to ~31-38% by step 100-300 (genuine, real progress); p_same correspondingly rises from ~1.5% to ~58-60% (the model strongly learns "keep the current speaker going"); but **p_handoff has been consistently LOW throughout (1.8%->4.2%->2.4%->4.2%) -- it never was high to begin with**, so the user's perceived "drastic drop from a good start" is a step-1 train-batch-noise artifact, not a real regression. The genuine, more important finding this confirms: BB is learning "don't overlap" reasonably well but has NOT yet learned "hand the floor to someone else" in any measurable way -- consistent with every other finding in this race. |

| — | — | 07-31 03:25 (real bug diagnosed) | **candidate69 (DD's search reference) FAILED with a genuine CUDA OOM** at step1600, 748 micro-batches into a train_slice -- verified the V100 clamp itself was NOT at fault (batch_size=1, max_flat_len=1536, gradient_checkpointing=True, `PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF=expandable_segments:True` all correctly applied). Traceback shows per-iteration time climbing steadily right before the crash (12.8s/it -> 69s/it over its last 8 steps) -- the classic signature of progressive CUDA memory fragmentation on V100's tighter 32GB, not an oversized config; higher-risk profile appears to be large lora_r (64) + longer lookback (128/192). Documented as a new standing rule (`.cursor/rules/v100-progressive-fragmentation-oom.mdc`) so future OOM failures in this family aren't misread as "bad hyperparameters" when reviewing search results, and so a climbing `s/it` in a personally-managed DGX arm's tqdm output is recognized as an early warning. Controller handled the failure gracefully on its own (candidate marked failed, slot freed, no crash) -- no code fix needed, monitoring-only finding. DD (269172, same recipe family, still healthy at step760+) is worth watching for the same pattern going forward. |

Confirmed its actual best result is NOT lost: `checkpoints/aug2026_cand_00050/rung1_step1500/` (the exact checkpoint that scored 9.905) is permanently protected by the earlier promotion-snapshot fix, and the leaderboard still shows its real score -- only its live continuation toward rung2/finalist died. Two occurrences of the same root cause on valuable candidates warranted a proactive fix rather than continued monitoring: added `--empty_cache_every N` (`training/config.py` + `main.py`, calls `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` every N optimizer steps, gated to CUDA only) to periodically defragment the allocator during long runs. Wired `--empty_cache_every 100` into `scripts/search/worker.py` (takes effect automatically on every future train_slice subprocess call -- no controller/worker-pool restart needed, since worker.py launches a fresh `main.py` subprocess per ticket) and as an overridable `EMPTY_CACHE_EVERY` (default 100) in `scripts/training/train_meld_ami_werewolf.sbatch` for future personal DGX launches. 226/226 tests still passing, no lints. Did not touch the 5 currently-running personal DGX arms (would require a disruptive resume; none are currently showing the fragmentation early-warning sign) -- the fix applies to all NEW launches from here on. |

| — | — | 07-31 07:39 (CRITICAL gap found: the OOM fix never actually took effect on the search side) | **candidate64 (the search's actual #1 leader) ALSO failed with the identical OOM signature**, and its failure timestamp (02:05 local) was AFTER the fix was supposedly deployed (~00:40 local) -- meaning the fix did not prevent it. Root cause: `scripts/search/worker.py` is a single **long-lived process** (`while True` loop, started once when the worker pool job launched on 07-30) that read its own source into memory at startup and never re-imports itself -- editing `worker.py` on disk has ZERO effect on already-running worker processes; only `main.py`/`training/config.py` changes take effect immediately (each `subprocess.run` execs a fresh interpreter). The `--empty_cache_every` flag was therefore never actually being passed by the live workers despite the file edit landing correctly hours earlier. Confirmed candidate64's real best result is still safe (checkpoints/promotion snapshots unaffected, same as candidate50/69). |

| — | **CRITICAL finding: Bazinga's entire processed dataset is 100% `test`-split (0 real train rows) -- both live Bazinga-ablation jobs were silently no-ops; cancelled** | 08-27 15:0x | While regenerating the final-run eval contract (below), found `bazinga` in the current manifest has ALL 11283 rows labeled `dataset_split=test`, zero `train`/`validation`. Root cause: the 08-25 partial-download issue (361/~370 episode files failed under `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`) left only 5 surviving episodes (`BattlestarGalactica.S01E01`, `TheBigBangTheory.S01E01-04`) -- `_split_for_identifier`'s season-based rule (`.Season01.Episode` anywhere -> `test`; `.Season02/03.Episode` -> `validation`; else -> `train`) is itself correctly designed for a FULL multi-season download, but with only Season01 episodes present, literally 100% of surviving rows land in `test`. Consequence, confirmed by reading `training/multi_source.py::load_interleaved_dataset`: when `split="train"` is requested and a source's post-filter row count is 0, that source is silently dropped from `parts`/`selected_names` entirely (no error) -- so `dataset_weights={"bazinga": 0.2, ...}` trains on **zero** actual Bazinga rows, silently renormalizing to the exact same effective ratio as the plain leader recipe. Worse: `load_interleaved_dataset` also has a `split="validation"` fallback (`if "validation" not in values and "test" in values: wanted="test"`) that pulls a source's nominally-SEALED `test` rows into the validation loss whenever it lacks real validation rows -- this is what actually caused the 45919-example validation blowup two entries above (misattributed there to "no chain/window dedup"; the real cause is this test-split fallback pulling in ALL 11283 Bazinga test rows, not a dedup gap). **Cancelled `data_abl_bazinga_s176106_retry` (274238, ~19h47m sunk) and `data_abl_bazinga_when2speak_s176106_retry` (274239, ~19h47m sunk)** -- confirmed via `.out` banners that 274238's effective weights (`{ami:0.4,meld:0.12,werewolf:0.28,bazinga:0.2}`) renormalize to EXACTLY `{ami:0.5,meld:0.15,werewolf:0.35}` once bazinga's 0 rows drop out (byte-identical to the already-extensively-validated leader mix -- ~38 wasted GPU-hours total for zero new information), and 274239 was redundant with the still-running `data_abl_when2speak_s176106` (274223) once its own bazinga contribution is removed. **Not fixed yet** (needs either a real re-download of the missing episodes across all seasons, or an explicit guard that treats an all-one-split source as a data quality error rather than silently proceeding) -- flagged as a follow-up; Bazinga is not part of the current best (`ami`/`meld`/`werewolf`-only) recipe so this does not affect the final-run launch below. Also worth a follow-up fix regardless of Bazinga: the validation-split test-fallback in `multi_source.py` should at minimum log a warning when it engages, since it can pull nominally-sealed `test` rows into a training run's loss reporting for ANY source that lacks real validation data. | CANCELLED (274238/239) | Re-download Bazinga's missing episodes before re-attempting this ablation; add a loud warning/guard for the validation test-fallback |

| — | **`worker.py` resume-guard bug: false train failures + Optuna contamination** | 08-07 10:36 (hourly checkup) | Found 3 of the first ~15 real phase-three candidates (`h100p3_cand_00006/00008/00009`) marked `failed` despite clean exits -- full log inspection (per `.cursor/rules/search-worker-handled-failures.mdc`, never trust a bare status) showed each hit `max_runtime_minutes=45` mid-training, checkpointed cleanly, then failed on their SECOND train-slice ticket's resume-compatibility guard with `refusing incompatible resume: {'unfreeze_n_layers': (None, 2)}`. **Root cause: my own bug from this session** -- the guard compared `previous.get('unfreeze_n_layers')` against the sampled value, but `main.py`'s saved `hyperparameters/<id>.json` only ever contains the real argparse field names `unfreeze_first_n_layers`/`unfreeze_last_n_layers`, never the controller-only synthetic `unfreeze_n_layers` key -- so `previous.get(...)` was ALWAYS `None`, guaranteeing every multi-slice resume for ANY candidate would falsely reject. This is systemic, not isolated: it would eventually have hit every candidate whose config makes a single 45-min slice insufficient to reach its rung's target_steps (larger batch/lora_r/lookback configs, exactly the slower architecture-search candidates). **Worse**: these 3 failures were told to Optuna as a real `score=0.0` (train-slice failures have no infra-vs-hyperparameter retry classifier analogous to `retryable_evaluation_failure`, which only covers `broad_evaluate` tickets) -- genuine false-zero contamination of the TPE study at trial numbers 5/7/8 (within/at the edge of the `tpe_startup_trials=8` random phase). Fixed `worker.py` to compare against the correct real field names instead of the synthetic key; added a regression test (`test_training_command_resume_guard_accepts_matching_unfreeze`) that would have caught this (the original test's fake hyperparameters JSON coincidentally used the same wrong key name, hiding the bug). Attempted to correct the 3 contaminated Optuna trials directly (`storage.set_trial_state_values`) but Optuna's RDB storage rejects updates to already-COMPLETE trials via the public API; judged a raw-SQL fix against the live, controller-shared SQLite file too risky for 3 early-startup-phase points and left uncorrected in Optuna's model -- flagged here for awareness if early TPE behavior looks skewed away from large-batch/high-lora_r/all_speakers regions without cause. Reset the 3 candidates' status to `ready_train` (+ `optuna_told=False` so their real outcome tells correctly this time) so they get genuine retries on their preserved checkpoints. Since `worker.py` is itself a long-lived per-search-dir process (same "needs restart" rule as controller/watchdog/pool_manager -- new to this project since it now also polls in a loop, not just execs main.py fresh), **restarted all 4 worker pools** (h100p3 primary+extra, `month_2026_08_phase2`, `month_2026_08_phase3`) after force-reclaiming their orphaned ticket claims; also had to redo the h100p3 controller/watchdog restart (kill-patch-restart pattern, stale lock cleared) to apply the candidate-status fix without the live process's next write cycle clobbering it. Verified: full suite 275/275, all 12 jobs (2 controllers, 2 watchdogs, 4 H100 + 15 V100 workers) RUNNING with zero tracebacks, all 3 candidates now `queued_train`. | FIXED | 3 trials' Optuna record remains a stale false-zero; monitor for skew |

| — | — | 07-21 14:24 (monitoring infra fix) | User reported (correctly) that the hourly wake mechanism was unreliable -- the `nohup ... & disown`-based background timer was completing and printing its sentinel on schedule, but was not reliably triggering a new agent turn to act on it (confirmed via terminal inspection: the sentinel WAS printed, ~20+ min before this checkup). Root cause: fully disowning the process likely drops it from the harness's own tracking, so its `notify_on_output`-style wake-up path has nothing to watch. Switched to keeping the timer as a normal (non-disowned) backgrounded shell job with an explicit `notify_on_output` pattern match on the sentinel, which the harness is documented to actively watch and notify on -- should be materially more reliable than the previous fire-and-forget approach. |
